The approved plan

I'll read the design source first.

REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339
SHARED_REDUCTION: seq_mac.add_out_reg (the accumulator the product is summed into; the block replaces both the product register and the accumulator register)
BLOCKS: 84 (one dsp48e2_macc per seq_mac instance; the module has 84 instances)
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance — CLK = clk; A = {11'b0, a_flopped} (16-bit unsigned, zero-extended to 27, A[26]=0); B = {2'b0, b_flopped} (16-bit unsigned, zero-extended to 18, B[17]=0); C = {31'b0, ab_sum_reg} where ab_sum_reg is a new 17-bit soft register holding a+b (zero-extended); OPMODE = (eltwise_mode & eltwise_add) ? 9'b110000000 : 9'b000100101; RSTA = RSTB = RSTP = reset; CEA2 = CEB2 = CEP = 1'b1; P[31:0] replaces the signal add_out_reg (feeds the existing mac_out down-cast); P[47:32] unused.

PLAN:

Candidate regions. There is one candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339 (o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier, 16x16 unsigned, 84 instances). It is converted. Nothing is left behind.

What the block absorbs. In seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846) the chain is: a_flopped/b_flopped → qmult → mul_out_reg → qadd (with the add_in1/add_in2 muxes) → add_out_reg. The block absorbs the multiply, mul_out_reg, the 32-bit adder, the two 32-bit muxes and add_out_reg. a_flopped/b_flopped, the mac_out down-cast (conv_layer.v:2823-2835) and the out register stay in soft logic.

Why the operand registers stay. The accumulator consumes a product that is two register stages behind the module inputs: add_out_reg(k) = add_out_reg(k-1) + mul_out_reg(k-1) and mul_out_reg(k-1) = a_flopped(k-2)*b_flopped(k-2) = a(k-3)*b(k-3). The block's own path is P(k) = P(k-1) + A2(k-1)*B2(k-1) with A2(k-1) = A(k-2), so A must carry a delayed one cycle, i.e. a_flopped. Hence A2/B2 take the role of the mul_out_reg stage and P takes the role of add_out_reg; the soft a_flopped/b_flopped registers remain and feed A/B. Driving A/B from a/b directly would make the product arrive one cycle early and is wrong. Shifting the whole accumulator one cycle earlier (so A/B could take a/b) is not possible either: P(k) is decided by OPMODE and C of cycle k-1, while add_out_reg(k+1) depends on reset and eltwise_mode & eltwise_add of cycle k — the control would have to be known a cycle before it exists.

The eltwise-add bypass. When eltwise_mode & eltwise_add holds, add_out_reg(k) = a_flopped(k-1) + b_flopped(k-1) (zero-extended, conv_layer.v:2807-2810). Instead of routing a_flopped + b_flopped into C combinationally (which would put a 16-bit adder in series with the 2.159 ns C-to-P path), add one soft register clocked with a_flopped/b_flopped:

reg [`DWIDTH:0] ab_sum_reg;          // 17 bits
always @(posedge clk)
  if (reset) ab_sum_reg <= 0; else ab_sum_reg <= {1'b0,a} + {1'b0,b};

ab_sum_reg(k) = reset(k-1) ? 0 : a(k-1)+b(k-1) = a_flopped(k) + b_flopped(k) exactly, because a_flopped and b_flopped are cleared by the same reset. C is then driven straight from a flip-flop, so the C stage is 0.060 + 2.159 ≈ 2.22 ns, the same as the block's internal stage, and the 16-bit add sits in the short input stage instead. C is driven unconditionally; on accumulate cycles OPMODE = 000100101 ignores it.

Per-cycle control. OPMODE is combinational from the module inputs (no added register):

  • eltwise_mode & eltwise_add low → 000100101, P <= P + A2*B2, reproducing add_out_reg <= add_out_reg + mul_out_reg.
  • eltwise_mode & eltwise_add high → 110000000, P <= C, reproducing add_out_reg <= {16'b0,a_flopped} + {16'b0,b_flopped}.
    Both are legal values, so OPMODE is never undefined, including during reset.

Reset and enables. RSTP = reset clears P at the edge that samples reset high and wins over CEP and over OPMODE (the model clears p before the ALU value is considered), matching add_out_reg <= 0. RSTA = RSTB = reset makes A2(k-1) = reset(k-2) ? 0 : a_flopped(k-2), so the product fed into P at edge k is reset(k-2) ? 0 : a_flopped(k-2)*b_flopped(k-2), which is exactly mul_out_reg(k-1) including its reset. The clock enables are all tied to 1 because none of the replaced registers has an enable. This holds for every reset, not only the first, and reset here is seq_mac's port, which the PE drives from reset || (eltwise_mode && ~ready_for_eltwise_op) (conv_layer.v:2647) — unchanged.

Width and signedness. Both operands are 16-bit unsigned, zero-extended to 27 and 18 bits, so the top bit of each multiplier operand is 0 as the unsigned rule requires. The product fits in 32 bits exactly. C is a 17-bit unsigned value zero-extended to 48. add_out_reg is 32 bits and wraps mod 2^32; P wraps mod 2^48, and truncating an accumulated sum of 48-bit adds to its low 32 bits gives the same sequence, so P[31:0] equals add_out_reg cycle for cycle. Nothing reads P[47:32].

Visibility. The only values that cross the block boundary are add_out_reg (via P[31:0], read by the mac_out down-cast) and nothing else: mul_out/mul_out_reg and add_out are read only by the logic the block absorbs (conv_layer.v:2793-2818), and a_flopped/b_flopped, which were also read by the eltwise bypass, remain in soft logic and are additionally reproduced through ab_sum_reg. qmult and qadd are instantiated only here (conv_layer.v:2793, 2810); leave their module definitions in the file, just drop the two instances.

Register stages, before and after. Before: a,b → [a_flopped/b_flopped] → multiply → [mul_out_reg] → add/mux → [add_out_reg] → down-cast → [out]: four stages. After: a,b → [a_flopped/b_flopped soft] → block A/B → [A2/B2] → multiply+ALU → [P] → down-cast → [out]: four stages. The bypass path before: a,b → [a_flopped/b_flopped] → add/mux → [add_out_reg]: two stages to the accumulator; after: a,b → add → [ab_sum_reg] → C → [P]: two stages to P. Latency from inputs to out is unchanged at four cycles, so the comparison is cycle-for-cycle.

Edit, concretely. In seq_mac: delete mul_out, mul_out_reg and its always block, add_out, add_in1, add_in2, add_out_reg and its always block, and the qmult/qadd instances. Add ab_sum_reg to the existing always block at conv_layer.v:2783-2791. Instantiate dsp48e2_macc as a black box (do not add a definition to conv_layer.v) with the mapping above, and add wire [2*DWIDTH-1:0] add_out_reg = dsp_p[2*DWIDTH-1:0]; so the mac_out expression (conv_layer.v:2823-2835) and the out register (conv_layer.v:2838-2844) are left textually unchanged. No other module is touched.

No ASSUMES: line: the rewrite is exact for all inputs, all reset patterns and from power-up (the block's A2, B2, P start at 0; the one added soft register, ab_sum_reg, is written at the first clock edge of the testbench's reset phase and can only reach P through C, which OPMODE selects only when eltwise_mode & eltwise_add is high, while RSTP clears P throughout that phase).

DESIGN_CHANGE: if seq_mac dropped the separate mul_out_reg pipeline stage (accumulating a_flopped*b_flopped directly, one cycle less latency), the block's A2/B2 could be driven from a/b and serve as a_flopped/b_flopped, removing 32 more soft flip-flops per PE (2688 across the 84 instances), since the eltwise-add bypass is already served by ab_sum_reg.

response

I'll read the files now.

ACCEPT

Checked the edited seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2848) against the plan and the rules.

Plan compliance. The edit touches only the regions the plan names: mul_out, mul_out_reg and its always block, add_out, add_in1, add_in2, add_out_reg and its always block, and the qmult/qadd instances are gone; ab_sum_reg was added to the existing always block (conv_layer.v:2781, 2787, 2791); the dsp48e2_macc instance is at 2804-2817; add_out_reg is reintroduced as a wire from dsp_p[31:0] (2819-2820) so the mac_out down-cast and the out register are textually unchanged. No dsp48e2_macc definition was added (black box, confirmed by grep). qmult/qadd module definitions are intact (2851-2868); nothing outside seq_mac changed, consistent with the subtree check in sim_run.log:3. 84 blocks matches the design: 12 live PEs per systolic_pe_matrix (four are commented out, 2573/2578/2583/2588) × 7 matmul_4x4_systolic instances.

Ports. All eleven inputs connected by name: CLK, A (11 zeros + 16-bit a_flopped = 27), B (2 zeros + 16 = 18), C (31 zeros + 17-bit ab_sum_reg = 48), OPMODE, RSTA/RSTB/RSTP = reset, CEA2/CEB2/CEP = 1. P drives only dsp_p → add_out_reg → the down-cast, as planned; P[47:32] unread.

Behavior. With A2(k) = reset(k-1) ? 0 : a_flopped(k-1) (same for B2), P(k+1) = P(k) + A2(k)*B2(k) reproduces add_out_reg(k+1) = add_out_reg(k) + mul_out_reg(k) including the reset of the deleted mul_out_reg stage; latency to out stays four cycles. OPMODE = 110000000 under eltwise_mode & eltwise_add gives P <= C = ab_sum_reg(k) = a_flopped(k)+b_flopped(k), matching the deleted mux/adder path exactly, carry included (17 bits kept). Both OPMODE values are legal, so P is never X by control. RSTP = reset clears P ahead of CEP and the ALU value in the model (sim_reference/dsp48e2_macc.v:53), matching add_out_reg <= 0 on every reset, not just the first; ab_sum_reg is cleared by the same reset that clears a_flopped/b_flopped, so the bypass value stays exact across mid-run resets (the mac_reset at conv_layer.v:2647 is unchanged).

Widths/signedness. Both operands zero-extended with A[26]=0, B[17]=0, so the signed 27x18 product equals the original unsigned 16x16 product exactly; P wrapping mod 2^48 is congruent mod 2^32 to add_out_reg, and the invariant is re-established on every load-C and reset cycle, so P[31:0] equals add_out_reg cycle for cycle.

Visibility. mul_out, mul_out_reg, add_out, add_in1/2 had no readers outside the absorbed logic; a_flopped/b_flopped remain in soft logic; add_out_reg is the only crossing value and is carried on P. Grep confirms no other reader of any of these names in the file.

One residual, consistent with the plan's stated reasoning and not a rule violation: ab_sum_reg is a new soft register, so before the first reset its value is X. It can reach P only through C, which OPMODE selects only when eltwise_mode & eltwise_add is high, and RSTP holds P at 0 throughout a reset phase; every mid-run reset re-clears it. The rewrite does not depend on its power-up value once the design has been reset, and the run reports x_count 0 on both the main run and the reset-between-phases diagnostic.

tool events 12 recorded, 0 error(s)

1	// dsp48e2_macc: behavioural model of the arch mode dsp48e2_macc_mode, which mimics the AMD DSP48E2 slice in the
2	// one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" operation mode (UG579 v1.7 Table 2-12), attributes AREG = BREG = 1,
3	// ACASCREG = BCASCREG = 1, MREG = 0, PREG = 1, CREG = 0, OPMODEREG = 0, USE_MULT = MULTIPLY, AMULTSEL = A,
4	// BMULTSEL = B, no pre-adder, no cascade, no pattern detector, ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000,
5	// CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0. Reference for every per-cycle question: AMD's simulation model
6	// /tools/Xilinx/Vivado/2024.1/data/verilog/src/unisims/DSP48E2.v (Vivado 2024.1; registers start at 0 in the
7	// initial block at lines 1178-1217), read in place and never copied.
8	// Per clock edge: A2 <= A if CEA2 (cleared by RSTA); B2 <= B if CEB2 (cleared by RSTB);
9	// P <= f(OPMODE, A2*B2, C, P) if CEP (cleared by RSTP). A, B to P: 2 cycles; C and OPMODE to P: 1 cycle
10	// (they are not registered). P wraps at 48 bits. OPMODE is restricted to the eight values below; on
11	// any other value at an edge that loads P, P becomes X (the mode does not define it).
12	// Signed two's complement throughout, as the real block; unsigned data must have a zero top bit on
13	// each operand. Every register starts at 0: AMD's simulation model starts its registers at 0 in the
14	// `initial` block(s) cited below (Vivado 2024.1). Resets are synchronous, active high, clear only the
15	// register(s) they name and win over that register's clock enable. Timing figures and tile area are
16	// this project's arch's own, not AMD's. One block per DSP tile.
17	module dsp48e2_macc (
18	    input CLK,
19	    input [26:0] A,
20	    input [17:0] B,
21	    input [47:0] C,
22	    input [8:0] OPMODE,
23	    input RSTA, RSTB, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2, CEP,
24	    output [47:0] P
25	);
26	    reg [26:0] a2 = 0;
27	    reg [17:0] b2 = 0;
28	    reg [47:0] p = 0;
29	    wire signed [44:0] m = $signed(a2) * $signed(b2);       // signed 27x18 -> 45, exact
30	    wire [47:0] mx = {{3{m[44]}}, m};
31	    // what P loads, as a function so that it is defined from time 0
32	    function [47:0] alu;
33	        input [8:0] opmode;
34	        input [47:0] p;
35	        input [47:0] C;
36	        input [47:0] mx;
37	        case (opmode)
38	            9'b000000101: alu = mx;            // multiply
39	            9'b000100101: alu = p + mx;        // multiply-accumulate (Z = P)
40	            9'b000110101: alu = C + mx;        // multiply-add (Z = C)
41	            9'b010110101: alu = p + C + mx;    // multiply-add with P (W = P, Z = C)
42	            9'b110000000: alu = C;             // load C (W = C)
43	            9'b000110000: alu = C;             // load C (Z = C)
44	            9'b010000000: alu = p;             // hold through the ALU (W = P)
45	            9'b000000000: alu = 48'd0;         // zero
46	            default:      alu = {48{1'bx}};    // not a value of this mode
47	        endcase
48	    endfunction
49	    wire [47:0] nxt = alu(OPMODE, p, C, mx);
50	    always @(posedge CLK) begin
51	        if (RSTA) a2 <= 0; else if (CEA2) a2 <= A;
52	        if (RSTB) b2 <= 0; else if (CEB2) b2 <= B;
53	        if (RSTP) p <= 0; else if (CEP) p <= nxt;
54	    end
55	    assign P = p;
56	endmodule

Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, conv_layer.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: dsp48e2_macc (precision INT27)
  • what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSP48E2 block in one configuration, "27x18 + C MULT/MACC, one slice" (UG579 v1.7 Table 2-12 (p44); OPMODE Tables 2-3 to 2-6 (p28)). It is not the whole DSP48E2: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, D = 0, A[29:27] = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: one slice per tile where AMD's tile has two; A is 27 bits: A[29:27] serve only the A:B path, which is outside the mode; OPMODE is restricted to the listed values; PCOUT, ACOUT, BCOUT, the carry, pattern-detect and XOR outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's.
  • computes: P = A*B + C. That is one of the forms the block takes: OPMODE chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
  • input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A[27], B[18], C[48], OPMODE[9], RSTA[1], RSTB[1], RSTP[1], CEA2[1], CEB2[1], CEP[1]
  • output ports: P[48]
  • arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each multiplier operand must be zero (UG579 p36: "The multiplier can emulate unsigned math by setting the MSB of an input operand to zero."). So an unsigned operand may be at most 26 bits on A and 17 bits on B; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. C and P are 48-bit values added modulo 2^48; extend a narrower addend to 48 bits by its own signedness. The result wraps at 48 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 48 bits (modulo 2^48), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on CLK, the block has exactly these registers. A2 loads A at a clock edge where CEA2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTA. B2 loads B at a clock edge where CEB2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTB. P loads the value OPMODE selects from A2*B2, C and P at a clock edge where CEP is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTP; the output P is this register. The block has no path around a register.
  • timing: A and B reach P 2 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in P after edge k+1); C and OPMODE have no register before the one that drives P: the value present at a clock edge is in P after that edge (1 cycle).

  • control OPMODE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P, with the C value present at that edge. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:

    • 000000101 (multiply): P <= A2*B2
    • 000100101 (multiply-accumulate): P <= P + A2*B2
    • 000110101 (multiply-add): P <= C + A2*B2
    • 010110101 (multiply-add with the running sum): P <= P + C + A2*B2
    • 110000000 (load C): P <= C
    • 000110000 (load C (second encoding)): P <= C
    • 010000000 (hold): P <= P
    • 000000000 (zero): P <= 0
    • any other value: a clock edge that loads P under it leaves P unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
  • resets and clock enables (resets RSTA, RSTB and RSTP; clock enables CEA2, CEB2 and CEP): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
  • power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so P is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; OPMODE may be a constant legal value.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block at A, B, RSTA, RSTB, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2 and CEP: a path ends at a register of the block (setup)0.019
into the block at C and OPMODE, which have no input register: the path runs on through the block's delay to the register behind P (delay + setup)2.140 + 0.019 = 2.159
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of P (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path into C and OPMODE is not cut at the block's pins: it runs through the block to the register behind P, so the logic that drives them and the block's delay are in one stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
Candidate regions

Multiplies in the unmodified conv_layer, counted in its packed netlist at seed 1: 84 on 84 DSP tiles, all in two_mult_18x19 mode (1 to a tile). The DSP tile count is the same at each of the five seeds. Multiplies in soft logic, if any, are not in this count.

Each row is one multiply of the design as elaborated from the source. An id names it by its module and by the name the elaborated design gives the multiply, which carries the file and the line; multiplies that come from one line (several written on it, or one inside a generate loop) differ in the number after the last $. An operand's bits are the bits it carries, the zero or sign extension to the width of the expression left out; signed or unsigned is how the multiply reads it. Instances is how many instances of the multiply's module the design holds. Nothing else about a multiply is listed: what its product feeds is in the source.

idsource lineleft operandright operandinstances
qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339assign o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier;i_multiplicand, 16 bits, unsignedi_multiplier, 16 bits, unsigned84
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's resets, clock enables or per-cycle control, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's resets (RSTA, RSTB and RSTP), clock enables (CEA2, CEB2 and CEP) and per-cycle control (OPMODE) so that "same behavior" holds after every reset of the design, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds in soft logic start unknown; do not rely on their initial value. The block's own registers start at 0, as its power-up line states.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Goal

Propose a correct rewrite onto the block. The loop measures it against the original design on area × delay; you are not asked to predict the result.

Output rules

What is currently built and measured

The kept candidate is first pass. conv_layer.v in this directory is the unmodified source as given to you, which your plan rewrites afresh; the kept candidate is the plan below, applied to that same source, and the diff below is that edit.

The kept candidate's plan

REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339
SHARED_REDUCTION: seq_mac.add_out_reg (the accumulator the product is summed into; the block replaces both the product register and the accumulator register)
BLOCKS: 84 (one dsp48e2_macc per seq_mac instance; the module has 84 instances)
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance — CLK = clk; A = {11'b0, a_flopped} (16-bit unsigned, zero-extended to 27, A[26]=0); B = {2'b0, b_flopped} (16-bit unsigned, zero-extended to 18, B[17]=0); C = {31'b0, ab_sum_reg} where ab_sum_reg is a new 17-bit soft register holding a+b (zero-extended); OPMODE = (eltwise_mode & eltwise_add) ? 9'b110000000 : 9'b000100101; RSTA = RSTB = RSTP = reset; CEA2 = CEB2 = CEP = 1'b1; P[31:0] replaces the signal add_out_reg (feeds the existing mac_out down-cast); P[47:32] unused.

PLAN:

Candidate regions. There is one candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339 (o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier, 16x16 unsigned, 84 instances). It is converted. Nothing is left behind.

What the block absorbs. In seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846) the chain is: a_flopped/b_flopped → qmult → mul_out_reg → qadd (with the add_in1/add_in2 muxes) → add_out_reg. The block absorbs the multiply, mul_out_reg, the 32-bit adder, the two 32-bit muxes and add_out_reg. a_flopped/b_flopped, the mac_out down-cast (conv_layer.v:2823-2835) and the out register stay in soft logic.

Why the operand registers stay. The accumulator consumes a product that is two register stages behind the module inputs: add_out_reg(k) = add_out_reg(k-1) + mul_out_reg(k-1) and mul_out_reg(k-1) = a_flopped(k-2)*b_flopped(k-2) = a(k-3)*b(k-3). The block's own path is P(k) = P(k-1) + A2(k-1)*B2(k-1) with A2(k-1) = A(k-2), so A must carry a delayed one cycle, i.e. a_flopped. Hence A2/B2 take the role of the mul_out_reg stage and P takes the role of add_out_reg; the soft a_flopped/b_flopped registers remain and feed A/B. Driving A/B from a/b directly would make the product arrive one cycle early and is wrong. Shifting the whole accumulator one cycle earlier (so A/B could take a/b) is not possible either: P(k) is decided by OPMODE and C of cycle k-1, while add_out_reg(k+1) depends on reset and eltwise_mode & eltwise_add of cycle k — the control would have to be known a cycle before it exists.

The eltwise-add bypass. When eltwise_mode & eltwise_add holds, add_out_reg(k) = a_flopped(k-1) + b_flopped(k-1) (zero-extended, conv_layer.v:2807-2810). Instead of routing a_flopped + b_flopped into C combinationally (which would put a 16-bit adder in series with the 2.159 ns C-to-P path), add one soft register clocked with a_flopped/b_flopped:

reg [`DWIDTH:0] ab_sum_reg;          // 17 bits
always @(posedge clk)
  if (reset) ab_sum_reg <= 0; else ab_sum_reg <= {1'b0,a} + {1'b0,b};

ab_sum_reg(k) = reset(k-1) ? 0 : a(k-1)+b(k-1) = a_flopped(k) + b_flopped(k) exactly, because a_flopped and b_flopped are cleared by the same reset. C is then driven straight from a flip-flop, so the C stage is 0.060 + 2.159 ≈ 2.22 ns, the same as the block's internal stage, and the 16-bit add sits in the short input stage instead. C is driven unconditionally; on accumulate cycles OPMODE = 000100101 ignores it.

Per-cycle control. OPMODE is combinational from the module inputs (no added register):

  • eltwise_mode & eltwise_add low → 000100101, P <= P + A2*B2, reproducing add_out_reg <= add_out_reg + mul_out_reg.
  • eltwise_mode & eltwise_add high → 110000000, P <= C, reproducing add_out_reg <= {16'b0,a_flopped} + {16'b0,b_flopped}.
    Both are legal values, so OPMODE is never undefined, including during reset.

Reset and enables. RSTP = reset clears P at the edge that samples reset high and wins over CEP and over OPMODE (the model clears p before the ALU value is considered), matching add_out_reg <= 0. RSTA = RSTB = reset makes A2(k-1) = reset(k-2) ? 0 : a_flopped(k-2), so the product fed into P at edge k is reset(k-2) ? 0 : a_flopped(k-2)*b_flopped(k-2), which is exactly mul_out_reg(k-1) including its reset. The clock enables are all tied to 1 because none of the replaced registers has an enable. This holds for every reset, not only the first, and reset here is seq_mac's port, which the PE drives from reset || (eltwise_mode && ~ready_for_eltwise_op) (conv_layer.v:2647) — unchanged.

Width and signedness. Both operands are 16-bit unsigned, zero-extended to 27 and 18 bits, so the top bit of each multiplier operand is 0 as the unsigned rule requires. The product fits in 32 bits exactly. C is a 17-bit unsigned value zero-extended to 48. add_out_reg is 32 bits and wraps mod 2^32; P wraps mod 2^48, and truncating an accumulated sum of 48-bit adds to its low 32 bits gives the same sequence, so P[31:0] equals add_out_reg cycle for cycle. Nothing reads P[47:32].

Visibility. The only values that cross the block boundary are add_out_reg (via P[31:0], read by the mac_out down-cast) and nothing else: mul_out/mul_out_reg and add_out are read only by the logic the block absorbs (conv_layer.v:2793-2818), and a_flopped/b_flopped, which were also read by the eltwise bypass, remain in soft logic and are additionally reproduced through ab_sum_reg. qmult and qadd are instantiated only here (conv_layer.v:2793, 2810); leave their module definitions in the file, just drop the two instances.

Register stages, before and after. Before: a,b → [a_flopped/b_flopped] → multiply → [mul_out_reg] → add/mux → [add_out_reg] → down-cast → [out]: four stages. After: a,b → [a_flopped/b_flopped soft] → block A/B → [A2/B2] → multiply+ALU → [P] → down-cast → [out]: four stages. The bypass path before: a,b → [a_flopped/b_flopped] → add/mux → [add_out_reg]: two stages to the accumulator; after: a,b → add → [ab_sum_reg] → C → [P]: two stages to P. Latency from inputs to out is unchanged at four cycles, so the comparison is cycle-for-cycle.

Edit, concretely. In seq_mac: delete mul_out, mul_out_reg and its always block, add_out, add_in1, add_in2, add_out_reg and its always block, and the qmult/qadd instances. Add ab_sum_reg to the existing always block at conv_layer.v:2783-2791. Instantiate dsp48e2_macc as a black box (do not add a definition to conv_layer.v) with the mapping above, and add wire [2*DWIDTH-1:0] add_out_reg = dsp_p[2*DWIDTH-1:0]; so the mac_out expression (conv_layer.v:2823-2835) and the out register (conv_layer.v:2838-2844) are left textually unchanged. No other module is touched.

No ASSUMES: line: the rewrite is exact for all inputs, all reset patterns and from power-up (the block's A2, B2, P start at 0; the one added soft register, ab_sum_reg, is written at the first clock edge of the testbench's reset phase and can only reach P through C, which OPMODE selects only when eltwise_mode & eltwise_add is high, while RSTP clears P throughout that phase).

DESIGN_CHANGE: if seq_mac dropped the separate mul_out_reg pipeline stage (accumulating a_flopped*b_flopped directly, one cycle less latency), the block's A2/B2 could be driven from a/b and serve as a_flopped/b_flopped, removing 32 more soft flip-flops per PE (2688 across the 84 instances), since the eltwise-add bypass is already served by ab_sum_reg.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of seq_mac on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v) and the block's behavioural model (dsp48e2_macc.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: conv_layer.v

response

I'll read the source to see what more can be moved into the block.

REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339
SHARED_REDUCTION: seq_mac.add_out_reg (the accumulator the product is summed into; the block replaces the product register, the adder and the accumulator register)
BLOCKS: 84
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance — CLK = clk; A = {11'b0, a_flopped} (16-bit unsigned, zero-extended to 27, A[26]=0); B = {2'b0, b_flopped} (16-bit unsigned, zero-extended to 18, B[17]=0); C = {31'b0, ab_sum} where ab_sum is the combinational 17-bit wire {1'b0,a_flopped} + {1'b0,b_flopped} (zero-extended to 48); OPMODE = (eltwise_mode & eltwise_add) ? 9'b110000000 : 9'b000100101; RSTA = RSTB = RSTP = reset; CEA2 = CEB2 = CEP = 1'b1; P[31:0] replaces the signal add_out_reg (feeds the existing mac_out down-cast); P[47:32] unused.

PLAN:

This is the kept first-pass rewrite with one change: the added soft register ab_sum_reg is deleted and C is driven combinationally from a_flopped/b_flopped. Everything else (block count, A/B/OPMODE/reset/enable mapping, what the block absorbs) is unchanged.

Candidate regions. One candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339 (o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier, 16x16 unsigned, 84 instances, conv_layer.v:2849-2857). It is converted, in all 84 seq_mac instances. Nothing is left.

Why this change. The measured critical path of the kept candidate (5.327 ns, 5.32229 ns period) is the ab_sum_reg adder I added last pass: a latch → new_n52548 → new_n52546 → $and~1163^Y~10 → $add~1232^ADD~174 (4 carry stages) → li03280 → lo03280.D. That is the seq_mac input path: a_mem_access_counter (conv_layer.v:2299-2311) → the a_data_valid comparators (conv_layer.v:2314-2319) → the qualifying AND a0_data = a_data[15:0] & {16{a_data_valid}} & {16{validity_mask_a_rows[0]}} (conv_layer.v:2325, bit 10 of the AND entering adder chunk 11) → the 17-bit {1'b0,a}+{1'b0,b} → its synchronous-reset mux LUT → the register. The a/b arrival at a PE is already 2.91 ns of AND/compare logic; putting a carry chain plus a reset mux behind it (1.313 + 0.136 + 0.660 + 0.153 = 2.26 ns) created a new worst path. Removing the register removes both the carry chain and the reset mux from that path (back to a → one reset-mux LUT → a_flopped, the original structure, ≈ 3.9 ns) and puts the adder on the C path instead, which starts at a flip-flop local to the same PE: 0.060 (FF) + route + ≈ 0.58 (17 carry stages at 0.034) + route + 2.159 (C through the block to the P register) ≈ 4 ns with normal routes, i.e. below the path it replaces. It also deletes 17 flip-flops and their 17 reset-mux LUTs per instance (1428 of each over the design).

Why driving C combinationally is exact. Write X(k) for a register's value after edge k and x@k for a combinational signal's value just before edge k. The original (conv_layer.v:2807-2818) is
add_out_reg(k) = (eltwise_mode & eltwise_add)@k ? a_flopped(k-1) + b_flopped(k-1) : add_out_reg(k-1) + mul_out_reg(k-1).
The eltwise term needs a_flopped(k-1) + b_flopped(k-1), which is exactly the value of the combinational wire a_flopped + b_flopped present just before edge k — the same value the register ab_sum_reg held there last pass (ab_sum_reg(k-1) = a@(k-1)+b@(k-1) = a_flopped(k-1)+b_flopped(k-1)). C is sampled at the edge that loads P (it has no input register), so C carrying a_flopped + b_flopped directly is the identical value, cycle for cycle, with one fewer register in between. The zero-extension of each 16-bit operand into the 17-bit sum reproduces {{16{1'b0}},a_flopped} + {{16{1'b0}},b_flopped} exactly (the original 32-bit qadd of two zero-extended 16-bit values has zero above bit 16). C is driven unconditionally; on accumulate cycles OPMODE = 000100101 ignores it. This also removes the one soft register the previous pass added, so no register of the rewrite starts unknown and no start-up argument is needed.

What the block absorbs. In seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846) the chain is a_flopped/b_flopped → qmult → mul_out_reg → the add_in1/add_in2 mux pair → qadd → add_out_reg. The block absorbs the multiply, mul_out_reg, the two 32-bit muxes, the 32-bit adder and add_out_reg. a_flopped/b_flopped, the new 17-bit ab_sum adder, the mac_out down-cast (conv_layer.v:2823-2835) and the out register stay in soft logic.

Why the operand registers stay. add_out_reg(k) = add_out_reg(k-1) + a@(k-3)*b@(k-3), while the block gives P(k) = P(k-1) + A2(k-1)*B2(k-1) with A2(k-1) = A(k-2). So A must carry a delayed one cycle, i.e. a_flopped; A2/B2 take the role of the mul_out_reg stage and P the role of add_out_reg. Driving A/B from a/b would make the product arrive one cycle early. Shifting the accumulator one cycle earlier (so A/B could take a/b) is impossible: P(k) is decided by the OPMODE and C present at edge k, while add_out_reg(k+1) depends on eltwise_mode & eltwise_add at edge k+1 — control that does not exist yet. Also keep the resets on a_flopped/b_flopped: the original forces mul_out_reg to 0 for the cycle after a reset through them, and RSTA/RSTB alone does not reproduce that.

Per-cycle control. OPMODE is combinational from the module inputs, no added register:

  • eltwise_mode & eltwise_add low → 000100101, P <= P + A2*B2, reproducing add_out_reg <= add_out_reg + mul_out_reg;
  • high → 110000000, P <= C, reproducing add_out_reg <= {16'b0,a_flopped} + {16'b0,b_flopped}.
    Both are legal values, so OPMODE is never an undefined encoding, including while reset is asserted.

Reset and enables. RSTP = reset clears P at the edge that samples reset high and wins over CEP and over OPMODE, matching add_out_reg <= 0. RSTA = RSTB = reset makes A2(k-1) = reset@(k-1) ? 0 : a_flopped(k-2), so the product summed at edge k equals mul_out_reg(k-1) including its reset. All three clock enables are tied to 1 because none of the replaced registers has an enable. This holds for every reset, not just the first; reset here is seq_mac's port, which the PE drives from reset || (eltwise_mode && ~ready_for_eltwise_op) (conv_layer.v:2647), unchanged.

Width, signedness, wrap. Both operands are 16-bit unsigned, zero-extended to 27 and 18 bits, so the top bit of each multiplier operand is 0, as the unsigned rule requires; the product fits in 32 bits exactly. C is a 17-bit unsigned value zero-extended to 48. add_out_reg is 32 bits and wraps mod 2^32; P wraps mod 2^48, and the low 32 bits of a chain of 48-bit adds are the same sequence, so P[31:0] equals add_out_reg cycle for cycle. Nothing reads P[47:32].

Visibility. The only value crossing the block boundary is add_out_reg, through P[31:0], read by the mac_out down-cast. mul_out, mul_out_reg, add_in1, add_in2 and add_out are read only by logic the block absorbs (conv_layer.v:2793-2818). a_flopped/b_flopped are read by the eltwise bypass as well, and they stay in soft logic, feeding both A/B and the ab_sum adder. qmult and qadd are instantiated only here (conv_layer.v:2793, 2810); leave their module definitions in the file and drop the two instances.

Register stages, before and after. MAC path before: a,b → [a_flopped/b_flopped] → multiply → [mul_out_reg] → mux+add → [add_out_reg] → down-cast → [out]: four stages. After: a,b → [a_flopped/b_flopped soft] → block A/B → [A2/B2] → multiply+ALU → [P] → down-cast → [out]: four stages. Eltwise-add path before: a,b → [a_flopped/b_flopped] → 17-bit add + mux → [add_out_reg]: two stages to the accumulator. After: a,b → [a_flopped/b_flopped] → 17-bit add → C → [P]: two stages to P. Latency from inputs to out is unchanged at four cycles, so the comparison is cycle for cycle.

Edit, concretely (against the unmodified conv_layer.v; it is the kept diff with ab_sum_reg replaced by a wire). In seq_mac: leave the a_flopped/b_flopped always block at conv_layer.v:2783-2791 exactly as it is; delete mul_out, mul_out_reg and its always block, add_out, add_in1, add_in2, add_out_reg and its always block, and the qmult/qadd instances; add

wire [`DWIDTH:0] ab_sum;                       // 17 bits, combinational
assign ab_sum = {1'b0, a_flopped} + {1'b0, b_flopped};

wire [8:0] dsp_opmode;
assign dsp_opmode = (eltwise_mode & eltwise_add) ? 9'b110000000 : 9'b000100101;

wire [47:0] dsp_p;

dsp48e2_macc mac_dsp_u1 (
  .CLK(clk),
  .A({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, a_flopped}),
  .B({{(18-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, b_flopped}),
  .C({{(48-`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}, ab_sum}),
  .OPMODE(dsp_opmode),
  .RSTA(reset), .RSTB(reset), .RSTP(reset),
  .CEA2(1'b1), .CEB2(1'b1), .CEP(1'b1),
  .P(dsp_p)
);

wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_out_reg;
assign add_out_reg = dsp_p[2*`DWIDTH-1:0];

(zero-extension widths: 11 bits on A, 2 on B, 31 on C). dsp48e2_macc is instantiated as a black box — do not add a definition to conv_layer.v. The mac_out expression (conv_layer.v:2823-2835) and the out register (conv_layer.v:2838-2844) are left textually unchanged. No other module is touched.

No ASSUMES: line: the rewrite is exact for all inputs, all reset patterns and from power-up — the block's A2, B2, P start at 0, and the rewrite adds no soft register at all.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if seq_mac dropped the separate mul_out_reg pipeline stage (accumulating a_flopped*b_flopped directly, one cycle less latency), the block's A2/B2 could be driven from a/b and serve as a_flopped/b_flopped, removing 32 soft flip-flops per PE (2688 across the 84 instances); the eltwise-add bypass would then need a/b combinationally on C.

Read completed /work/sim_reference/dsp48e2_macc.v

 "file_path": "/work/sim_reference/dsp48e2_macc.v"

output
1	// dsp48e2_macc: behavioural model of the arch mode dsp48e2_macc_mode, which mimics the AMD DSP48E2 slice in the
2	// one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" operation mode (UG579 v1.7 Table 2-12), attributes AREG = BREG = 1,
3	// ACASCREG = BCASCREG = 1, MREG = 0, PREG = 1, CREG = 0, OPMODEREG = 0, USE_MULT = MULTIPLY, AMULTSEL = A,
4	// BMULTSEL = B, no pre-adder, no cascade, no pattern detector, ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000,
5	// CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0. Reference for every per-cycle question: AMD's simulation model
6	// /tools/Xilinx/Vivado/2024.1/data/verilog/src/unisims/DSP48E2.v (Vivado 2024.1; registers start at 0 in the
7	// initial block at lines 1178-1217), read in place and never copied.
8	// Per clock edge: A2 <= A if CEA2 (cleared by RSTA); B2 <= B if CEB2 (cleared by RSTB);
9	// P <= f(OPMODE, A2*B2, C, P) if CEP (cleared by RSTP). A, B to P: 2 cycles; C and OPMODE to P: 1 cycle
10	// (they are not registered). P wraps at 48 bits. OPMODE is restricted to the eight values below; on
11	// any other value at an edge that loads P, P becomes X (the mode does not define it).
12	// Signed two's complement throughout, as the real block; unsigned data must have a zero top bit on
13	// each operand. Every register starts at 0: AMD's simulation model starts its registers at 0 in the
14	// `initial` block(s) cited below (Vivado 2024.1). Resets are synchronous, active high, clear only the
15	// register(s) they name and win over that register's clock enable. Timing figures and tile area are
16	// this project's arch's own, not AMD's. One block per DSP tile.
17	module dsp48e2_macc (
18	    input CLK,
19	    input [26:0] A,
20	    input [17:0] B,
21	    input [47:0] C,
22	    input [8:0] OPMODE,
23	    input RSTA, RSTB, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2, CEP,
24	    output [47:0] P
25	);
26	    reg [26:0] a2 = 0;
27	    reg [17:0] b2 = 0;
28	    reg [47:0] p = 0;
29	    wire signed [44:0] m = $signed(a2) * $signed(b2);       // signed 27x18 -> 45, exact
30	    wire [47:0] mx = {{3{m[44]}}, m};
31	    // what P loads, as a function so that it is defined from time 0
32	    function [47:0] alu;
33	        input [8:0] opmode;
34	        input [47:0] p;
35	        input [47:0] C;
36	        input [47:0] mx;
37	        case (opmode)
38	            9'b000000101: alu = mx;            // multiply
39	            9'b000100101: alu = p + mx;        // multiply-accumulate (Z = P)
40	            9'b000110101: alu = C + mx;        // multiply-add (Z = C)
41	            9'b010110101: alu = p + C + mx;    // multiply-add with P (W = P, Z = C)
42	            9'b110000000: alu = C;             // load C (W = C)
43	            9'b000110000: alu = C;             // load C (Z = C)
44	            9'b010000000: alu = p;             // hold through the ALU (W = P)
45	            9'b000000000: alu = 48'd0;         // zero
46	            default:      alu = {48{1'bx}};    // not a value of this mode
47	        endcase
48	    endfunction
49	    wire [47:0] nxt = alu(OPMODE, p, C, mx);
50	    always @(posedge CLK) begin
51	        if (RSTA) a2 <= 0; else if (CEA2) a2 <= A;
52	        if (RSTB) b2 <= 0; else if (CEB2) b2 <= B;
53	        if (RSTP) p <= 0; else if (CEP) p <= nxt;
54	    end
55	    assign P = p;
56	endmodule

prompt
Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: dsp48e2_macc (precision INT27)
  • what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSP48E2 block in one configuration, "27x18 + C MULT/MACC, one slice" (UG579 v1.7 Table 2-12 (p44); OPMODE Tables 2-3 to 2-6 (p28)). It is not the whole DSP48E2: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, D = 0, A[29:27] = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: one slice per tile where AMD's tile has two; A is 27 bits: A[29:27] serve only the A:B path, which is outside the mode; OPMODE is restricted to the listed values; PCOUT, ACOUT, BCOUT, the carry, pattern-detect and XOR outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's.
  • computes: P = A*B + C. That is one of the forms the block takes: OPMODE chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
  • input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A[27], B[18], C[48], OPMODE[9], RSTA[1], RSTB[1], RSTP[1], CEA2[1], CEB2[1], CEP[1]
  • output ports: P[48]
  • arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each multiplier operand must be zero (UG579 p36: "The multiplier can emulate unsigned math by setting the MSB of an input operand to zero."). So an unsigned operand may be at most 26 bits on A and 17 bits on B; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. C and P are 48-bit values added modulo 2^48; extend a narrower addend to 48 bits by its own signedness. The result wraps at 48 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 48 bits (modulo 2^48), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on CLK, the block has exactly these registers. A2 loads A at a clock edge where CEA2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTA. B2 loads B at a clock edge where CEB2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTB. P loads the value OPMODE selects from A2*B2, C and P at a clock edge where CEP is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTP; the output P is this register. The block has no path around a register.
  • timing: A and B reach P 2 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in P after edge k+1); C and OPMODE have no register before the one that drives P: the value present at a clock edge is in P after that edge (1 cycle).

  • control OPMODE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P, with the C value present at that edge. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:

    • 000000101 (multiply): P <= A2*B2
    • 000100101 (multiply-accumulate): P <= P + A2*B2
    • 000110101 (multiply-add): P <= C + A2*B2
    • 010110101 (multiply-add with the running sum): P <= P + C + A2*B2
    • 110000000 (load C): P <= C
    • 000110000 (load C (second encoding)): P <= C
    • 010000000 (hold): P <= P
    • 000000000 (zero): P <= 0
    • any other value: a clock edge that loads P under it leaves P unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
  • resets and clock enables (resets RSTA, RSTB and RSTP; clock enables CEA2, CEB2 and CEP): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
  • power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so P is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; OPMODE may be a constant legal value.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block at A, B, RSTA, RSTB, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2 and CEP: a path ends at a register of the block (setup)0.019
into the block at C and OPMODE, which have no input register: the path runs on through the block's delay to the register behind P (delay + setup)2.140 + 0.019 = 2.159
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of P (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path into C and OPMODE is not cut at the block's pins: it runs through the block to the register behind P, so the logic that drives them and the block's delay are in one stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The design source is conv_layer.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

Multiplies in the unmodified conv_layer, counted in its packed netlist at seed 1: 84 on 84 DSP tiles, all in two_mult_18x19 mode (1 to a tile). The DSP tile count is the same at each of the five seeds. Multiplies in soft logic, if any, are not in this count.

Each row is one multiply of the design as elaborated from the source. An id names it by its module and by the name the elaborated design gives the multiply, which carries the file and the line; multiplies that come from one line (several written on it, or one inside a generate loop) differ in the number after the last $. An operand's bits are the bits it carries, the zero or sign extension to the width of the expression left out; signed or unsigned is how the multiply reads it. Instances is how many instances of the multiply's module the design holds. Nothing else about a multiply is listed: what its product feeds is in the source.

idsource lineleft operandright operandinstances
qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339assign o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier;i_multiplicand, 16 bits, unsignedi_multiplier, 16 bits, unsigned84
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's resets, clock enables or per-cycle control, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's resets (RSTA, RSTB and RSTP), clock enables (CEA2, CEB2 and CEP) and per-cycle control (OPMODE) so that "same behavior" holds after every reset of the design, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds in soft logic start unknown; do not rely on their initial value. The block's own registers start at 0, as its power-up line states.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically, and your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
What is currently built and measured

The kept candidate is first pass. conv_layer.v in this directory is the unmodified source as given to you, which the plan under review rewrites afresh; the kept candidate is the plan below, applied to that same source, and the diff below is that edit.

The kept candidate's plan

REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339
SHARED_REDUCTION: seq_mac.add_out_reg (the accumulator the product is summed into; the block replaces both the product register and the accumulator register)
BLOCKS: 84 (one dsp48e2_macc per seq_mac instance; the module has 84 instances)
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance — CLK = clk; A = {11'b0, a_flopped} (16-bit unsigned, zero-extended to 27, A[26]=0); B = {2'b0, b_flopped} (16-bit unsigned, zero-extended to 18, B[17]=0); C = {31'b0, ab_sum_reg} where ab_sum_reg is a new 17-bit soft register holding a+b (zero-extended); OPMODE = (eltwise_mode & eltwise_add) ? 9'b110000000 : 9'b000100101; RSTA = RSTB = RSTP = reset; CEA2 = CEB2 = CEP = 1'b1; P[31:0] replaces the signal add_out_reg (feeds the existing mac_out down-cast); P[47:32] unused.

PLAN:

Candidate regions. There is one candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339 (o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier, 16x16 unsigned, 84 instances). It is converted. Nothing is left behind.

What the block absorbs. In seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846) the chain is: a_flopped/b_flopped → qmult → mul_out_reg → qadd (with the add_in1/add_in2 muxes) → add_out_reg. The block absorbs the multiply, mul_out_reg, the 32-bit adder, the two 32-bit muxes and add_out_reg. a_flopped/b_flopped, the mac_out down-cast (conv_layer.v:2823-2835) and the out register stay in soft logic.

Why the operand registers stay. The accumulator consumes a product that is two register stages behind the module inputs: add_out_reg(k) = add_out_reg(k-1) + mul_out_reg(k-1) and mul_out_reg(k-1) = a_flopped(k-2)*b_flopped(k-2) = a(k-3)*b(k-3). The block's own path is P(k) = P(k-1) + A2(k-1)*B2(k-1) with A2(k-1) = A(k-2), so A must carry a delayed one cycle, i.e. a_flopped. Hence A2/B2 take the role of the mul_out_reg stage and P takes the role of add_out_reg; the soft a_flopped/b_flopped registers remain and feed A/B. Driving A/B from a/b directly would make the product arrive one cycle early and is wrong. Shifting the whole accumulator one cycle earlier (so A/B could take a/b) is not possible either: P(k) is decided by OPMODE and C of cycle k-1, while add_out_reg(k+1) depends on reset and eltwise_mode & eltwise_add of cycle k — the control would have to be known a cycle before it exists.

The eltwise-add bypass. When eltwise_mode & eltwise_add holds, add_out_reg(k) = a_flopped(k-1) + b_flopped(k-1) (zero-extended, conv_layer.v:2807-2810). Instead of routing a_flopped + b_flopped into C combinationally (which would put a 16-bit adder in series with the 2.159 ns C-to-P path), add one soft register clocked with a_flopped/b_flopped:

reg [`DWIDTH:0] ab_sum_reg;          // 17 bits
always @(posedge clk)
  if (reset) ab_sum_reg <= 0; else ab_sum_reg <= {1'b0,a} + {1'b0,b};

ab_sum_reg(k) = reset(k-1) ? 0 : a(k-1)+b(k-1) = a_flopped(k) + b_flopped(k) exactly, because a_flopped and b_flopped are cleared by the same reset. C is then driven straight from a flip-flop, so the C stage is 0.060 + 2.159 ≈ 2.22 ns, the same as the block's internal stage, and the 16-bit add sits in the short input stage instead. C is driven unconditionally; on accumulate cycles OPMODE = 000100101 ignores it.

Per-cycle control. OPMODE is combinational from the module inputs (no added register):

  • eltwise_mode & eltwise_add low → 000100101, P <= P + A2*B2, reproducing add_out_reg <= add_out_reg + mul_out_reg.
  • eltwise_mode & eltwise_add high → 110000000, P <= C, reproducing add_out_reg <= {16'b0,a_flopped} + {16'b0,b_flopped}.
    Both are legal values, so OPMODE is never undefined, including during reset.

Reset and enables. RSTP = reset clears P at the edge that samples reset high and wins over CEP and over OPMODE (the model clears p before the ALU value is considered), matching add_out_reg <= 0. RSTA = RSTB = reset makes A2(k-1) = reset(k-2) ? 0 : a_flopped(k-2), so the product fed into P at edge k is reset(k-2) ? 0 : a_flopped(k-2)*b_flopped(k-2), which is exactly mul_out_reg(k-1) including its reset. The clock enables are all tied to 1 because none of the replaced registers has an enable. This holds for every reset, not only the first, and reset here is seq_mac's port, which the PE drives from reset || (eltwise_mode && ~ready_for_eltwise_op) (conv_layer.v:2647) — unchanged.

Width and signedness. Both operands are 16-bit unsigned, zero-extended to 27 and 18 bits, so the top bit of each multiplier operand is 0 as the unsigned rule requires. The product fits in 32 bits exactly. C is a 17-bit unsigned value zero-extended to 48. add_out_reg is 32 bits and wraps mod 2^32; P wraps mod 2^48, and truncating an accumulated sum of 48-bit adds to its low 32 bits gives the same sequence, so P[31:0] equals add_out_reg cycle for cycle. Nothing reads P[47:32].

Visibility. The only values that cross the block boundary are add_out_reg (via P[31:0], read by the mac_out down-cast) and nothing else: mul_out/mul_out_reg and add_out are read only by the logic the block absorbs (conv_layer.v:2793-2818), and a_flopped/b_flopped, which were also read by the eltwise bypass, remain in soft logic and are additionally reproduced through ab_sum_reg. qmult and qadd are instantiated only here (conv_layer.v:2793, 2810); leave their module definitions in the file, just drop the two instances.

Register stages, before and after. Before: a,b → [a_flopped/b_flopped] → multiply → [mul_out_reg] → add/mux → [add_out_reg] → down-cast → [out]: four stages. After: a,b → [a_flopped/b_flopped soft] → block A/B → [A2/B2] → multiply+ALU → [P] → down-cast → [out]: four stages. The bypass path before: a,b → [a_flopped/b_flopped] → add/mux → [add_out_reg]: two stages to the accumulator; after: a,b → add → [ab_sum_reg] → C → [P]: two stages to P. Latency from inputs to out is unchanged at four cycles, so the comparison is cycle-for-cycle.

Edit, concretely. In seq_mac: delete mul_out, mul_out_reg and its always block, add_out, add_in1, add_in2, add_out_reg and its always block, and the qmult/qadd instances. Add ab_sum_reg to the existing always block at conv_layer.v:2783-2791. Instantiate dsp48e2_macc as a black box (do not add a definition to conv_layer.v) with the mapping above, and add wire [2*DWIDTH-1:0] add_out_reg = dsp_p[2*DWIDTH-1:0]; so the mac_out expression (conv_layer.v:2823-2835) and the out register (conv_layer.v:2838-2844) are left textually unchanged. No other module is touched.

No ASSUMES: line: the rewrite is exact for all inputs, all reset patterns and from power-up (the block's A2, B2, P start at 0; the one added soft register, ab_sum_reg, is written at the first clock edge of the testbench's reset phase and can only reach P through C, which OPMODE selects only when eltwise_mode & eltwise_add is high, while RSTP clears P throughout that phase).

DESIGN_CHANGE: if seq_mac dropped the separate mul_out_reg pipeline stage (accumulating a_flopped*b_flopped directly, one cycle less latency), the block's A2/B2 could be driven from a/b and serve as a_flopped/b_flopped, removing 32 more soft flip-flops per PE (2688 across the 84 instances), since the eltwise-add bypass is already served by ab_sum_reg.

The plan

I'll read the source to see what more can be moved into the block.

REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339
SHARED_REDUCTION: seq_mac.add_out_reg (the accumulator the product is summed into; the block replaces the product register, the adder and the accumulator register)
BLOCKS: 84
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance — CLK = clk; A = {11'b0, a_flopped} (16-bit unsigned, zero-extended to 27, A[26]=0); B = {2'b0, b_flopped} (16-bit unsigned, zero-extended to 18, B[17]=0); C = {31'b0, ab_sum} where ab_sum is the combinational 17-bit wire {1'b0,a_flopped} + {1'b0,b_flopped} (zero-extended to 48); OPMODE = (eltwise_mode & eltwise_add) ? 9'b110000000 : 9'b000100101; RSTA = RSTB = RSTP = reset; CEA2 = CEB2 = CEP = 1'b1; P[31:0] replaces the signal add_out_reg (feeds the existing mac_out down-cast); P[47:32] unused.

PLAN:

This is the kept first-pass rewrite with one change: the added soft register ab_sum_reg is deleted and C is driven combinationally from a_flopped/b_flopped. Everything else (block count, A/B/OPMODE/reset/enable mapping, what the block absorbs) is unchanged.

Candidate regions. One candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339 (o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier, 16x16 unsigned, 84 instances, conv_layer.v:2849-2857). It is converted, in all 84 seq_mac instances. Nothing is left.

Why this change. The measured critical path of the kept candidate (5.327 ns, 5.32229 ns period) is the ab_sum_reg adder I added last pass: a latch → new_n52548 → new_n52546 → $and~1163^Y~10 → $add~1232^ADD~174 (4 carry stages) → li03280 → lo03280.D. That is the seq_mac input path: a_mem_access_counter (conv_layer.v:2299-2311) → the a_data_valid comparators (conv_layer.v:2314-2319) → the qualifying AND a0_data = a_data[15:0] & {16{a_data_valid}} & {16{validity_mask_a_rows[0]}} (conv_layer.v:2325, bit 10 of the AND entering adder chunk 11) → the 17-bit {1'b0,a}+{1'b0,b} → its synchronous-reset mux LUT → the register. The a/b arrival at a PE is already 2.91 ns of AND/compare logic; putting a carry chain plus a reset mux behind it (1.313 + 0.136 + 0.660 + 0.153 = 2.26 ns) created a new worst path. Removing the register removes both the carry chain and the reset mux from that path (back to a → one reset-mux LUT → a_flopped, the original structure, ≈ 3.9 ns) and puts the adder on the C path instead, which starts at a flip-flop local to the same PE: 0.060 (FF) + route + ≈ 0.58 (17 carry stages at 0.034) + route + 2.159 (C through the block to the P register) ≈ 4 ns with normal routes, i.e. below the path it replaces. It also deletes 17 flip-flops and their 17 reset-mux LUTs per instance (1428 of each over the design).

Why driving C combinationally is exact. Write X(k) for a register's value after edge k and x@k for a combinational signal's value just before edge k. The original (conv_layer.v:2807-2818) is
add_out_reg(k) = (eltwise_mode & eltwise_add)@k ? a_flopped(k-1) + b_flopped(k-1) : add_out_reg(k-1) + mul_out_reg(k-1).
The eltwise term needs a_flopped(k-1) + b_flopped(k-1), which is exactly the value of the combinational wire a_flopped + b_flopped present just before edge k — the same value the register ab_sum_reg held there last pass (ab_sum_reg(k-1) = a@(k-1)+b@(k-1) = a_flopped(k-1)+b_flopped(k-1)). C is sampled at the edge that loads P (it has no input register), so C carrying a_flopped + b_flopped directly is the identical value, cycle for cycle, with one fewer register in between. The zero-extension of each 16-bit operand into the 17-bit sum reproduces {{16{1'b0}},a_flopped} + {{16{1'b0}},b_flopped} exactly (the original 32-bit qadd of two zero-extended 16-bit values has zero above bit 16). C is driven unconditionally; on accumulate cycles OPMODE = 000100101 ignores it. This also removes the one soft register the previous pass added, so no register of the rewrite starts unknown and no start-up argument is needed.

What the block absorbs. In seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846) the chain is a_flopped/b_flopped → qmult → mul_out_reg → the add_in1/add_in2 mux pair → qadd → add_out_reg. The block absorbs the multiply, mul_out_reg, the two 32-bit muxes, the 32-bit adder and add_out_reg. a_flopped/b_flopped, the new 17-bit ab_sum adder, the mac_out down-cast (conv_layer.v:2823-2835) and the out register stay in soft logic.

Why the operand registers stay. add_out_reg(k) = add_out_reg(k-1) + a@(k-3)*b@(k-3), while the block gives P(k) = P(k-1) + A2(k-1)*B2(k-1) with A2(k-1) = A(k-2). So A must carry a delayed one cycle, i.e. a_flopped; A2/B2 take the role of the mul_out_reg stage and P the role of add_out_reg. Driving A/B from a/b would make the product arrive one cycle early. Shifting the accumulator one cycle earlier (so A/B could take a/b) is impossible: P(k) is decided by the OPMODE and C present at edge k, while add_out_reg(k+1) depends on eltwise_mode & eltwise_add at edge k+1 — control that does not exist yet. Also keep the resets on a_flopped/b_flopped: the original forces mul_out_reg to 0 for the cycle after a reset through them, and RSTA/RSTB alone does not reproduce that.

Per-cycle control. OPMODE is combinational from the module inputs, no added register:

  • eltwise_mode & eltwise_add low → 000100101, P <= P + A2*B2, reproducing add_out_reg <= add_out_reg + mul_out_reg;
  • high → 110000000, P <= C, reproducing add_out_reg <= {16'b0,a_flopped} + {16'b0,b_flopped}.
    Both are legal values, so OPMODE is never an undefined encoding, including while reset is asserted.

Reset and enables. RSTP = reset clears P at the edge that samples reset high and wins over CEP and over OPMODE, matching add_out_reg <= 0. RSTA = RSTB = reset makes A2(k-1) = reset@(k-1) ? 0 : a_flopped(k-2), so the product summed at edge k equals mul_out_reg(k-1) including its reset. All three clock enables are tied to 1 because none of the replaced registers has an enable. This holds for every reset, not just the first; reset here is seq_mac's port, which the PE drives from reset || (eltwise_mode && ~ready_for_eltwise_op) (conv_layer.v:2647), unchanged.

Width, signedness, wrap. Both operands are 16-bit unsigned, zero-extended to 27 and 18 bits, so the top bit of each multiplier operand is 0, as the unsigned rule requires; the product fits in 32 bits exactly. C is a 17-bit unsigned value zero-extended to 48. add_out_reg is 32 bits and wraps mod 2^32; P wraps mod 2^48, and the low 32 bits of a chain of 48-bit adds are the same sequence, so P[31:0] equals add_out_reg cycle for cycle. Nothing reads P[47:32].

Visibility. The only value crossing the block boundary is add_out_reg, through P[31:0], read by the mac_out down-cast. mul_out, mul_out_reg, add_in1, add_in2 and add_out are read only by logic the block absorbs (conv_layer.v:2793-2818). a_flopped/b_flopped are read by the eltwise bypass as well, and they stay in soft logic, feeding both A/B and the ab_sum adder. qmult and qadd are instantiated only here (conv_layer.v:2793, 2810); leave their module definitions in the file and drop the two instances.

Register stages, before and after. MAC path before: a,b → [a_flopped/b_flopped] → multiply → [mul_out_reg] → mux+add → [add_out_reg] → down-cast → [out]: four stages. After: a,b → [a_flopped/b_flopped soft] → block A/B → [A2/B2] → multiply+ALU → [P] → down-cast → [out]: four stages. Eltwise-add path before: a,b → [a_flopped/b_flopped] → 17-bit add + mux → [add_out_reg]: two stages to the accumulator. After: a,b → [a_flopped/b_flopped] → 17-bit add → C → [P]: two stages to P. Latency from inputs to out is unchanged at four cycles, so the comparison is cycle for cycle.

Edit, concretely (against the unmodified conv_layer.v; it is the kept diff with ab_sum_reg replaced by a wire). In seq_mac: leave the a_flopped/b_flopped always block at conv_layer.v:2783-2791 exactly as it is; delete mul_out, mul_out_reg and its always block, add_out, add_in1, add_in2, add_out_reg and its always block, and the qmult/qadd instances; add

wire [`DWIDTH:0] ab_sum;                       // 17 bits, combinational
assign ab_sum = {1'b0, a_flopped} + {1'b0, b_flopped};

wire [8:0] dsp_opmode;
assign dsp_opmode = (eltwise_mode & eltwise_add) ? 9'b110000000 : 9'b000100101;

wire [47:0] dsp_p;

dsp48e2_macc mac_dsp_u1 (
  .CLK(clk),
  .A({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, a_flopped}),
  .B({{(18-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, b_flopped}),
  .C({{(48-`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}, ab_sum}),
  .OPMODE(dsp_opmode),
  .RSTA(reset), .RSTB(reset), .RSTP(reset),
  .CEA2(1'b1), .CEB2(1'b1), .CEP(1'b1),
  .P(dsp_p)
);

wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_out_reg;
assign add_out_reg = dsp_p[2*`DWIDTH-1:0];

(zero-extension widths: 11 bits on A, 2 on B, 31 on C). dsp48e2_macc is instantiated as a black box — do not add a definition to conv_layer.v. The mac_out expression (conv_layer.v:2823-2835) and the out register (conv_layer.v:2838-2844) are left textually unchanged. No other module is touched.

No ASSUMES: line: the rewrite is exact for all inputs, all reset patterns and from power-up — the block's A2, B2, P start at 0, and the rewrite adds no soft register at all.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if seq_mac dropped the separate mul_out_reg pipeline stage (accumulating a_flopped*b_flopped directly, one cycle less latency), the block's A2/B2 could be driven from a/b and serve as a_flopped/b_flopped, removing 32 soft flip-flops per PE (2688 across the 84 instances); the eltwise-add bypass would then need a/b combinationally on C.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of seq_mac on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v) and the block's behavioural model (dsp48e2_macc.v) are in sim_reference/.

prompt
Task

Edit conv_layer.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: dsp48e2_macc (precision INT27)
  • what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSP48E2 block in one configuration, "27x18 + C MULT/MACC, one slice" (UG579 v1.7 Table 2-12 (p44); OPMODE Tables 2-3 to 2-6 (p28)). It is not the whole DSP48E2: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, D = 0, A[29:27] = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: one slice per tile where AMD's tile has two; A is 27 bits: A[29:27] serve only the A:B path, which is outside the mode; OPMODE is restricted to the listed values; PCOUT, ACOUT, BCOUT, the carry, pattern-detect and XOR outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's.
  • computes: P = A*B + C. That is one of the forms the block takes: OPMODE chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
  • input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A[27], B[18], C[48], OPMODE[9], RSTA[1], RSTB[1], RSTP[1], CEA2[1], CEB2[1], CEP[1]
  • output ports: P[48]
  • arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each multiplier operand must be zero (UG579 p36: "The multiplier can emulate unsigned math by setting the MSB of an input operand to zero."). So an unsigned operand may be at most 26 bits on A and 17 bits on B; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. C and P are 48-bit values added modulo 2^48; extend a narrower addend to 48 bits by its own signedness. The result wraps at 48 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 48 bits (modulo 2^48), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on CLK, the block has exactly these registers. A2 loads A at a clock edge where CEA2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTA. B2 loads B at a clock edge where CEB2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTB. P loads the value OPMODE selects from A2*B2, C and P at a clock edge where CEP is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTP; the output P is this register. The block has no path around a register.
  • timing: A and B reach P 2 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in P after edge k+1); C and OPMODE have no register before the one that drives P: the value present at a clock edge is in P after that edge (1 cycle).

  • control OPMODE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P, with the C value present at that edge. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:

    • 000000101 (multiply): P <= A2*B2
    • 000100101 (multiply-accumulate): P <= P + A2*B2
    • 000110101 (multiply-add): P <= C + A2*B2
    • 010110101 (multiply-add with the running sum): P <= P + C + A2*B2
    • 110000000 (load C): P <= C
    • 000110000 (load C (second encoding)): P <= C
    • 010000000 (hold): P <= P
    • 000000000 (zero): P <= 0
    • any other value: a clock edge that loads P under it leaves P unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
  • resets and clock enables (resets RSTA, RSTB and RSTP; clock enables CEA2, CEB2 and CEP): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
  • power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so P is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; OPMODE may be a constant legal value.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block at A, B, RSTA, RSTB, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2 and CEP: a path ends at a register of the block (setup)0.019
into the block at C and OPMODE, which have no input register: the path runs on through the block's delay to the register behind P (delay + setup)2.140 + 0.019 = 2.159
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of P (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path into C and OPMODE is not cut at the block's pins: it runs through the block to the register behind P, so the logic that drives them and the block's delay are in one stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
  • Multiply in the approved plan: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339 (module qmult).
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's resets, clock enables or per-cycle control, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's resets (RSTA, RSTB and RSTP), clock enables (CEA2, CEB2 and CEP) and per-cycle control (OPMODE) so that "same behavior" holds after every reset of the design, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds in soft logic start unknown; do not rely on their initial value. The block's own registers start at 0, as its power-up line states.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh conv_layer.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
